++y) {\n            for (size_t x = 0; x < nx; ++x) {\n                const size_t idx = idx3(x, y, z, nx, ny);\n                cnew[idx] = cold[idx] + dt * D * \n                           computeLaplacian(mu, nx, ny, nz, dx, dy, dz, x, y, z);\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n// Initialize concentration field\nvoid initializeConcentration(std::vector<double>& c, const size_t nx, const size_t ny, const size_t nz) {\n    const size_t vol = nx * ny * nz;\n    \n    for (size_t z = 0; z < nz; ++z) {\n        for (size_t y = 0; y < ny; ++y) {\n            for (size_t x = 0; x < nx; ++x) {\n                const size_t idx = idx3(x, y, z, nx, ny);\n                // Generate pseudo-random value in [-1, 1]\n                const size_t linear_id = z * (nx * ny) + y * nx + x;\n                const double pseudo = ((((linear_id + 1) * 1299709) % vol) / static_cast<double>(vol));\n                c[idx] = -1.0 + 2.0 * pseudo;\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}\n\nbool validateResult(const std::vector<double>& c, [[maybe_unused]] const size_t nx, [[maybe_unused]] const size_t ny, [[maybe_unused]] const size_t nz) {\n    // Check for NaN or Inf\n    for (const auto& values are in reasonable range for concentration field\n    // After Cahn-Hilliard evolution, values should typically remain bounded\n    double minVal = c[0];\n    double maxVal = c[0];\n    for (const auto& val : c) {\n        minVal = std::min(minVal, val);\n        maxVal = std::max(maxVal, val);\n    }\n    \n    printf(\"Concentration range: [%.6f, %.6f]\\n\", minVal, maxVal);\n    \n    // Values should generally stay within reasonable bounds\n    if (maxVal > 10.0 || minVal < -10.0) {\n        printf(\"Validation failed: values out of expected range\\n\");\n        return false;\n k < k1; k += gridDim.z) {\n        const long gz = z0 + k;\n        const long zpOff = (gz < nz - 1) ? plane : 0;\n        const long znOff = (gz > 0) ? -plane : 0;\n        const long inPlane = (long)y * nx + x;\n        const double* p = c + (k + CHALO) * plane + inPlane;\n        const double cv = *p;\n        mu[(k + MHALO) * plane + inPlane] =\n            4.5 * ((cv + 1.0) * e_AA + (cv - 1.0) * e_BB - 2.0 * cv * e_AB)\n            + 3.0 * cv + cv * cv * cv\n            - gamma * laplacian(p, x, y, nx, ny, zpOff, znOff, dx, dy, dz);\n    }\n}\n\n// Cahn-Hilliard update for local planes [k0, k1)\n__global__ void cahnHilliardUpdateKernel(double* __restrict__ cnew, const double* __restrict__ cold,\n                                         const double* __restrict__ mu,\n                                         const int nx, const int ny, const long nz, const long z0,\n                                         const long k0, const long k1,\n                                         const double D, const double dt,\n                                         const double dx, const double dy, const double dz) {\n    const int x = blockIdx.x * BX + threadIdx.x;\n    const int y = blockIdx.y * BY + threadIdx.y;\n    if (x >= nx || y >= ny) return;\n    const long plane = (long)nx * ny;\n    for (long k = k0 + blockIdx.z; MPI_Barrier(MPI_COMM_WORLD);\n    \n    auto end = std::chrono::high_resolution_clock::now();\n    auto duration = std::chrono::duration_cast<std::chrono::milliseconds>(end - start);\n    \n    if 'p' points to the centre value,\n// in-plane clamping uses global x/y, z clamping is resolved by the caller via\n// the zp/zn plane offsets (0 at the global boundary).\n__device__ __forceinline__ double laplacian(const double* __restrict__ p, const int x, const int y,\n                                            const int nx, const int ny, const long zpOff, const long znOff,\n                                            const double dx, const double dy, const double dz) {\n    const int xpOff = (x < nx - 1) ? 1 : 0;\n    const int xnOff = (x > 0) ? -1 : 0;\n    const int ypOff = (y < ny - 1) ? nx : 0;\n    const int ynOff = (y > 0) ? -nx : 0;\n    const double cv = __ldg(p);\n    const double cxx = (__ldg(p + xpOff) + __ldg(p + xnOff) - 2.0 * cv) / (dx * dx);\n    const double cyy = (__ldg(p + ypOff) + __ldg(p + ynOff) - 2.0 * cv) / (dy * dy);\n    const double czz = (__ldg(p + zpOff) + __ldg(p + znOff) - 2.0 * cv) / (dz * dz);\n    return cxx + cyy + czz;\n}\n\n// Compute chemical potential for local planes [k0, k1). Local plane k is\n// global plane z0 + k. c has CHALO halo planes, mu has MHALO halo planes.\n__global__ void chemicalPotentialKernel(const double* __restrict__ c, double* __restrict__ mu,\n                                        const int nx, const int ny, const long nz, const long z0,\n                                        const long k0, const long k1,\n                                        const double dx, const double dy, const double dz,\n                                        const double gamma, const double e_AA, const double e_BB,\n                                        const double e_AB) {\n    const int x = blockIdx.x * BX + threadIdx.x;\n    const int y = blockIdx.y * BY + threadIdx.y;\n    if (x >= nx || y >= ny) return;\n    const long plane = (long)nx * ny;\n    for (long k = k0 + blockIdx.z; k < k1; k += gridDim.z) {\n        const long gz = z0 + k;\n        const long zpOff = (gz < nz - 1) ? plane : 0;\n        const long znOff = (gz > 0) ? -plane : 0;\n        const long inPlane = (long)y * nx + x;\n        const double* p = c + (k + CHALO) * plane + inPlane;\n        const double cv = *p;\n        // 4.5 * ((cv + 1) * e_AA + (cv - 1) * e_BB - 2 * cv * e_AB) + 3 * cv + cv^3 - gamma * lap,\n        // with the fused multiply-adds the host compiler generates for the reference build\n        const double energy = fma(-(2.0 * cv), e_AB, fma(cv + 1.0, e_AA, (cv - 1.0) * e_BB));\n        const double local = fma(cv * cv, cv, fma(4.5, energy, 3.0 * cv));\n        mu[(k + MHALO) * plane + inPlane] =\n            fma(-gamma, laplacian(p, x, y, nx, ny, zpOff, znOff, dx, dy, dz), local);\n    }\n}\n\n// Cahn-Hilliard update for local planes [k0, k1)\n__global__ void cahnHilliardUpdateKernel(double* __restrict__ cnew, const double* __restrict__ cold,\n                                         const double* __restrict__ mu,\n                                         const int nx, const int ny, const long nz, const long z0,\n                                         const long k0, const long k1,\n                                         const double D, const double dt,\n                                         const double dx, const double dy, const double dz) {\n    const int x = blockIdx.x * BX + threadIdx.x;\n    const int y = blockIdx.y * BY + threadIdx.y;\n    if (x >= nx || y >= ny) return;\n    const long plane = (long)nx * ny;\n    for (long k = k0 + blockIdx.z; k < k1; k += gridDim.z) {\n        const long gz = z0 + k;\n        const long zpOff = (gz < nz - 1) ? std::min(1L, nzl) : 0;\n    const long inHi = hasNeighbours ? std::max(inLo, nzl - 1) : nzl;\n\n    auto launchMu = [&](const long k0, const long k1) {\n        if (k1 <= k0) return;\n        chemicalPotentialKernel<<<gridFor(inx, iny, k1 - k0), dim3(BX, BY), 0, sComp>>>(\n            d_cold, d_mu, inx, iny, lnz, z0, k0, k1, dx, dy, dz, gamma, e_AA, e_BB, e_AB);\n    };\n    \n    // Run simulation\n    if (root) printf(\"Running Cahn-Hilliard simulation...\\n\");\n                launchMu(std::max(inHi, inLo), muHi);\n            }\n\n            // Update concentration\n            cahnHilliardUpdateKernel<<<gridFor(inx, iny, nzl), dim3(BX, BY), 0, sComp>>>(\n                d_cnew, d_cold, d_mu, inx, iny, lnz, z0, 0, nzl, D, dt, dx, dy, dz);\n            \n            // Swap buffers\n            std::swap(d_cold, d_cnew);","stderr":"","interrupted":false,"isImage":false,"noOutputExpected":false}}

{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5-5","id":"msg_011CfhfMFjHMLaBYt5Rkb9KP","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"I parallelized the benchmark with MPI, CUDA and OpenMP, changing only `cahn_hilliard.cpp` and `CMakeLists.txt`. The executable is still `cahn_hilliard`. Results are **bitwise identical** to the original CMake build: the printed result hash matched on 5 grid shapes, including odd sizes and a single-plane grid. I checked 1, 2, 3, 4, 7 and 8 ranks (7 and 8 run more than one rank per GPU). `-v`, `-r`, `-h` and the exit codes behave as before.\n\n**How the work is split**\n- **MPI:** the grid is cut into slabs along z, one MPI process per GPU. Each process picks a GPU on its own node. Every process gets at least 2 planes; any extra processes sit idle but still take part in the final gather. For `-r` and `-v`, the full field is collected on rank 0.\n- **CUDA:** everything (initialization, chemical potential, update) runs on the GPU.\n  - Each time step there is a single exchange of 2 boundary planes with each neighbour. That gives each GPU enough data to compute both stencil passes, so one exchange per step is enough instead of two.\n  - The exchange overlaps with computation. The interior of both kernels runs while boundary planes go GPU → pinned host memory → MPI → GPU. Only thin slabs at the slab edges are computed after the data arrives. The receive buffers alternate between steps to avoid a race that I found and fixed during testing.\n  - The installed Open MPI can't send directly from GPU memory, so data goes through host memory.\n- **OpenMP:** used for the host-side validation scans (NaN/Inf check and min/max). There is little other CPU work, so this is its only use.\n\n**How results stay bitwise identical**\n- GCC combines some multiply-adds into single FMA instructions when it builds the original. I read its assembly and wrote the same `fma()` calls explicitly in the kernels. Automatic FMA is turned off in nvcc so it adds no others.\n- Dividing by `dx*dx` is replaced with multiplying by `1/(dx*dx)` only when the program confirms that gives exactly the same result (true for this benchmark's spacing of 1). Otherwise it divides as before. Removing those divisions made a single GPU about 2.2× faster.\n\n**Performance** (RTX 3090s, MCellUpdates/s)\n\n| Grid | Serial CPU | 1 GPU | 2 GPUs | 4 GPUs |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| 256³ | ~160 | 7,034 | 12,428 | 17,033 |\n| 512³ | — | 7,193 | 14,069 | 27,475 (3.8×) |\n\nA single GPU is close to the 3090's double-precision limit. These are timings from single runs.\n\n**Build:** CMake now needs CUDA, MPI, OpenMP and CMake 3.18 or newer. It compiles the `.cpp` with nvcc for the local GPU type (`CUDA_ARCHITECTURES native`); set `-DCMAKE_CUDA_ARCHITECTURES=…` to target a different GPU. Run it as, for example, `mpirun -np <number of GPUs> ./cahn_hilliard -x 512`. I deleted my temporary build directory afterwards."}],"container":null,"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":866,"cache_read_input_tokens":69667,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":866},"output_tokens":6,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"input_transformations":[],"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"cd44ec02-eb2b-4b55-9d5c-587533003a7f","uuid":"5064db2c-02c4-43e8-9247-0f5acfae0e97","timestamp":"2026-10-04T18:22:17.374Z","request_id":"req_011CfhfMFQRzyotRChJtgta2"}
